                                The Free Web-Making
                                  Mega Handbook
                        183 researched resource entries + 430 original code recipes

                                     Research snapshot: 2026-08-07




  A step-by-step, license-aware field guide to HTML, CSS, JavaScript, responsive UI, animation,
 component sources, templates, playgrounds, generators, icons, and screenshot-inspired builds.




Free Web-Making Mega Handbook                                                              Page 1
 How to use this handbook
 The directory is a researched snapshot, not a claim that the changing Internet can be literally exhausted. It
 intentionally distinguishes open source, free tools, freemium resources, playgrounds with author-controlled licenses,
 and social/comment-gated code leads.

 The 430 numbered recipes are original instructional examples written for this handbook. External pages are
 research/reference links; the code recipes below are not scraped copies.

 For each recipe: run it, change one value, break it deliberately, restore it, then test keyboard, narrow viewport, zoom,
 reduced motion, and failure states when relevant. That turns a snippet into a skill.

 License survival rules
 - Free to view is not automatically free to republish.

 - A public GitHub repository needs a LICENSE file before you assume reuse rights.

 - A comment/DM/Telegram download does not itself grant a license.

 - Freemium sites can have free components beside paid or commercially restricted material.

 - Record source URL, version/commit, license, required notices, and dependencies before shipping third-party code.

 Study roadmap
 - HTML foundations: snippets 001-072

 - CSS micro-recipes: snippets 073-222

 - JavaScript browser recipes: snippets 223-380

 - UI mini-projects: snippets 381-430

 HTML foundations
                                Recipes 001-072




Free Web-Making Mega Handbook                     Page 14
 Snippet 001 - Minimal HTML5 document
 Goal: Use minimal html5 document as a small, standards-first building block.
   <!doctype html>
   <html lang="en">
   <head>
     <meta charset="utf-8">
     <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
     <title>Page title</title>
   </head>
   <body>
     <main>Hello, web.</main>
   </body>
   </html>


 Step by step
 1. Paste the snippet into the document where its meaning belongs.

 2. Replace placeholder text/URLs with real content; keep semantic relationships such as labels, headings, and
 captions intact.

 3. Inspect the rendered DOM and use only attributes that communicate real state or purpose.

 4. Test with keyboard navigation and a narrow viewport before styling it heavily.

 Verify: Reload with DevTools open; verify the element is present, keyboard reachable when interactive, and
 announced sensibly by accessibility inspection tools.

 Gotcha: HTML semantics are behavior, not decoration. Do not add ARIA that contradicts native element behavior, and
 never put untrusted HTML directly into the page.

 1. Paste the snippet into the document where its meaning belongs.

 2. Replace placeholder text/URLs with real content; keep semantic relationships such as labels, headings, and
 captions intact.

 3. Inspect the rendered DOM and use only attributes that communicate real state or purpose.

 4. Test with keyboard navigation and a narrow viewport before styling it heavily.

 Verify: Reload with DevTools open; verify the element is present, keyboard reachable when interactive, and
 announced sensibly by accessibility inspection tools.

 Gotcha: HTML semantics are behavior, not decoration. Do not add ARIA that contradicts native element behavior, and
 never put untrusted HTML directly into the page.

Free Web-Making Mega Handbook                     Page 87
 Snippet 073 - Universal border-box sizing - core
 Goal: Apply the smallest useful version of universal border-box sizing.
   .universal-border-box-sizing {
     box-sizing: border-box;
   }


 Step by step
 1. Add the class to the element you want to change.

 2. Copy the rule into your stylesheet after base/reset styles.

 3. Change the values one at a time so you can see which declaration controls the effect.

 4. Check at 320px, a tablet width, and a wide desktop width.

 Verify: In DevTools, toggle each declaration off and on; confirm the rule changes only the intended box or text.

 Gotcha: A micro-rule can interact with parent layout, writing mode, overflow, or user preferences. Test it in context
 instead of assuming the isolated demo guarantees production behavior.




Free Web-Making Mega Handbook                                                                                       Page 88
 Snippet 074 - Universal border-box sizing - responsive opt-in
 Goal: Introduce universal border-box sizing only when at least 48rem of viewport width is available.
   .universal-border-box-sizing {
     /* safe/default state first */
   }

   @media (min-width: 48rem) {
     .universal-border-box-sizing {
       box-sizing: border-box;
     }
   }


 Step by step
 1. Start with a usable small-screen state outside the media query.

 2. Place the enhancement inside the min-width query.

 3. Resize through the boundary rather than testing only two fixed screenshots.

 4. If the behavior depends on component space instead of viewport space, consider a container query instead.

 Verify: Drag the responsive preview slowly across 48rem and confirm content never overlaps, clips, or becomes
 unreachable.

 Gotcha: A breakpoint is a content decision, not a device label. If 48rem does not match the content's failure point,
 move the breakpoint to where the layout actually needs it.

 unreachable.

 Snippet 223 - Select one element
 Goal: Implement the browser-side task: select one element.
   const card = document.querySelector('.card');
   console.log(card);


 Step by step
 1. Put the code in a module script or a JavaScript file loaded after the relevant markup exists.

 2. Replace example selectors, endpoints, IDs, and data with values from your page.

 3. Handle the empty/error state before adding animation or visual polish.

 4. Exercise the action with mouse, keyboard, touch when relevant, and inspect the console/network panel for failures.

 Verify: Trigger both the normal path and at least one failure/empty path; verify there are no uncaught exceptions and
 the UI state stays understandable.

 Gotcha: Browser APIs differ in permissions and support. Feature-detect optional APIs, do not inject untrusted strings
 with innerHTML, and do not expose secrets in client-side JavaScript.

 Snippet 288 - Debounce a function
 Goal: Implement the browser-side task: debounce a function.
   function debounce(fn, wait = 200) {
     let timer;
     return (...args) => {
        clearTimeout(timer);
        timer = setTimeout(() => fn(...args), wait);
     };
   }

 Snippet 293 - Lazy-load on intersection
 Goal: Implement the browser-side task: lazy-load on intersection.
   const observer = new IntersectionObserver(([entry], obs) => {
     if (!entry.isIntersecting) return;
     entry.target.src = entry.target.dataset.src;
     obs.unobserve(entry.target);
   });
   document.querySelectorAll('img[data-src]').forEach((img) => observer.observe(img));

 Goal: Implement the browser-side task: parse a url safely.
   function parseHttpUrl(value) {
     try {
       const url = new URL(value);
       return ['http:', 'https:'].includes(url.protocol) ? url : null;
     } catch { return null; }
   }

 Snippet 346 - Respect device pixel ratio on canvas
 Goal: Implement the browser-side task: respect device pixel ratio on canvas.
   const canvas = document.querySelector('canvas');
   const dpr = devicePixelRatio || 1;
   if (canvas) {
     const { width, height } = canvas.getBoundingClientRect();
     canvas.width = Math.round(width * dpr);
     canvas.height = Math.round(height * dpr);
     canvas.getContext('2d')?.scale(dpr, dpr);
   }


 Step by step
 1. Put the code in a module script or a JavaScript file loaded after the relevant markup exists.

 2. Replace example selectors, endpoints, IDs, and data with values from your page.

Free Web-Making Mega Handbook                     Page 397
 Snippet 381 - Screenshot-inspired 3D photo gallery
 Goal: Use perspective, horizontal cards, and keyboard buttons without copying the pictured creator's code.
   <!-- HTML -->
   <section class="demo screenshot-inspired-3d-photo-gallery" aria-labelledby="screenshot-
     inspired-3d-photo-gallery-title">
     <div class="demo__content">
       <p class="demo__eyebrow">Mini-project 01</p>
       <h2 id="screenshot-inspired-3d-photo-gallery-title">Screenshot-inspired 3D photo
         gallery</h2>
       <p>Replace this text with the real content for the component.</p>
       <button class="demo__action" type="button" aria-pressed="false">Try interaction</button>
       <p class="demo__status" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p>
     </div>
   </section>

   /* CSS */
   .screenshot-inspired-3d-photo-gallery {
     --accent: hsl(47 78% 56%);
     display: grid;
     place-items: center;
     min-block-size: min(70dvh, 42rem);
     padding: clamp(1rem, 5vw, 4rem);
     background: linear-gradient(135deg, color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 18%, #fff), #fff);
   }
   .screenshot-inspired-3d-photo-gallery .demo__content {
     inline-size: min(100%, 50rem);
     padding: clamp(1.25rem, 4vw, 3rem);
     border: 1px solid #0002;
     border-radius: 1.25rem;
     background: #fff;
     box-shadow: 0 18px 60px #0002;
   }
   .screenshot-inspired-3d-photo-gallery .demo__action { min-block-size: 44px; padding-inline:
     1rem; }
   @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference) {
     .screenshot-inspired-3d-photo-gallery .demo__content { transition: transform 180ms ease; }
     .screenshot-inspired-3d-photo-gallery:has(.demo__action[aria-pressed="true"]) .demo__content
        { transform: translateY(-4px); }
   }

   // JavaScript
   const root1 = document.querySelector('.screenshot-inspired-3d-photo-gallery');
   const button1 = root1?.querySelector('.demo__action');
   const status1 = root1?.querySelector('.demo__status');
   button1?.addEventListener('click', () => {
     const next = button1.getAttribute('aria-pressed') !== 'true';
     button1.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(next));
     if (status1) status1.textContent = next ? 'Demo state enabled.' : 'Demo state disabled.';
   });


 Step by step
 1. Create a blank document and paste the HTML portion into body, the CSS portion into a stylesheet, and the
 JavaScript portion into a module script.

 2. Keep the semantic title, real button, status region, focus behavior, and 44px minimum control target while
 changing the visual design.

 3. Replace the generic content with the content/controls named in the project title; use native HTML elements before
 inventing custom roles.

 4. Test the component at 320px and 1440px, at 200% zoom, with keyboard only, and with reduced-motion enabled.

 5. Only after the interaction works without animation should you add richer transforms, images, or library effects.

 Verify: Click the action, press it with Enter and Space, and inspect aria-pressed/status changes. Resize and enable
 prefers-reduced-motion to verify the layout and motion fallback.

 Gotcha: These mini-projects are original teaching scaffolds inspired by common interface ideas (including the
 screenshots), not copied source from the creators. For production, specialize the structure and behavior to the
 component's actual semantics.

   <!-- HTML -->
   <section class="demo hero-with-call-to-action" aria-labelledby="hero-with-call-to-action-
     title">
     <div class="demo__content">
       <p class="demo__eyebrow">Mini-project 12</p>
       <h2 id="hero-with-call-to-action-title">Hero with call-to-action</h2>
       <p>Replace this text with the real content for the component.</p>
       <button class="demo__action" type="button" aria-pressed="false">Try interaction</button>
       <p class="demo__status" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p>
     </div>
   </section>

   /* CSS */
   .hero-with-call-to-action {
     --accent: hsl(204 78% 56%);
     display: grid;
     place-items: center;
     min-block-size: min(70dvh, 42rem);
     padding: clamp(1rem, 5vw, 4rem);
     background: linear-gradient(135deg, color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 18%, #fff), #fff);
   }
   .hero-with-call-to-action .demo__content {
     inline-size: min(100%, 50rem);
     padding: clamp(1.25rem, 4vw, 3rem);
     border: 1px solid #0002;
     border-radius: 1.25rem;
     background: #fff;
     box-shadow: 0 18px 60px #0002;
   }
   .hero-with-call-to-action .demo__action { min-block-size: 44px; padding-inline: 1rem; }
   @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference) {
     .hero-with-call-to-action .demo__content { transition: transform 180ms ease; }
     .hero-with-call-to-action:has(.demo__action[aria-pressed="true"]) .demo__content {
       transform: translateY(-4px); }
   }

   // JavaScript
   const root12 = document.querySelector('.hero-with-call-to-action');
   const button12 = root12?.querySelector('.demo__action');
   const status12 = root12?.querySelector('.demo__status');
   button12?.addEventListener('click', () => {
     const next = button12.getAttribute('aria-pressed') !== 'true';
     button12.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(next));
     if (status12) status12.textContent = next ? 'Demo state enabled.' : 'Demo state disabled.';
   });


 Step by step
 1. Create a blank document and paste the HTML portion into body, the CSS portion into a stylesheet, and the
 JavaScript portion into a module script.

 2. Keep the semantic title, real button, status region, focus behavior, and 44px minimum control target while
 changing the visual design.

 3. Replace the generic content with the content/controls named in the project title; use native HTML elements before
 inventing custom roles.

 4. Test the component at 320px and 1440px, at 200% zoom, with keyboard only, and with reduced-motion enabled.

 5. Only after the interaction works without animation should you add richer transforms, images, or library effects.

 Verify: Click the action, press it with Enter and Space, and inspect aria-pressed/status changes. Resize and enable
 prefers-reduced-motion to verify the layout and motion fallback.

 Gotcha: These mini-projects are original teaching scaffolds inspired by common interface ideas (including the
 screenshots), not copied source from the creators. For production, specialize the structure and behavior to the
 component's actual semantics.

 component's actual semantics.




Free Web-Making Mega Handbook                                                                                      Page 443
 Snippet 427 - Lazy media gallery
 Goal: Combine loading=lazy with fixed dimensions to reduce layout shift.
   <!-- HTML -->
   <section class="demo lazy-media-gallery" aria-labelledby="lazy-media-gallery-title">
     <div class="demo__content">
       <p class="demo__eyebrow">Mini-project 47</p>
       <h2 id="lazy-media-gallery-title">Lazy media gallery</h2>
       <p>Replace this text with the real content for the component.</p>
       <button class="demo__action" type="button" aria-pressed="false">Try interaction</button>
       <p class="demo__status" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p>
     </div>
   </section>

   /* CSS */
   .lazy-media-gallery {
     --accent: hsl(49 78% 56%);
     display: grid;
     place-items: center;
     min-block-size: min(70dvh, 42rem);
     padding: clamp(1rem, 5vw, 4rem);
     background: linear-gradient(135deg, color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 18%, #fff), #fff);
   }
   .lazy-media-gallery .demo__content {
     inline-size: min(100%, 50rem);
     padding: clamp(1.25rem, 4vw, 3rem);
     border: 1px solid #0002;
     border-radius: 1.25rem;
     background: #fff;
     box-shadow: 0 18px 60px #0002;
   }
   .lazy-media-gallery .demo__action { min-block-size: 44px; padding-inline: 1rem; }
   @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference) {
     .lazy-media-gallery .demo__content { transition: transform 180ms ease; }
     .lazy-media-gallery:has(.demo__action[aria-pressed="true"]) .demo__content { transform:
       translateY(-4px); }
   }

   // JavaScript
   const root47 = document.querySelector('.lazy-media-gallery');
   const button47 = root47?.querySelector('.demo__action');
   const status47 = root47?.querySelector('.demo__status');
   button47?.addEventListener('click', () => {
     const next = button47.getAttribute('aria-pressed') !== 'true';
     button47.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(next));
     if (status47) status47.textContent = next ? 'Demo state enabled.' : 'Demo state disabled.';
   });


 Step by step
 1. Create a blank document and paste the HTML portion into body, the CSS portion into a stylesheet, and the
 JavaScript portion into a module script.

 2. Keep the semantic title, real button, status region, focus behavior, and 44px minimum control target while
 changing the visual design.

 3. Replace the generic content with the content/controls named in the project title; use native HTML elements before
 inventing custom roles.

 4. Test the component at 320px and 1440px, at 200% zoom, with keyboard only, and with reduced-motion enabled.

 5. Only after the interaction works without animation should you add richer transforms, images, or library effects.

 Verify: Click the action, press it with Enter and Space, and inspect aria-pressed/status changes. Resize and enable
 prefers-reduced-motion to verify the layout and motion fallback.

 Gotcha: These mini-projects are original teaching scaffolds inspired by common interface ideas (including the
 screenshots), not copied source from the creators. For production, specialize the structure and behavior to the
 component's actual semantics.

Free Web-Making Mega Handbook                                                                                      Page 447
 Part III - Build discipline checklist
 1. Start with valid semantic HTML and a keyboard-usable interaction.

 2. Add CSS from the small-screen/default state outward; use Grid/Flexbox for layout and transforms/opacity for
 motion.

 3. Add JavaScript only for state/behavior that HTML and CSS cannot express cleanly.

 4. Treat user input, URLs, storage, network data, and copied third-party snippets as untrusted until validated.

 5. Test at 200% zoom, narrow width, keyboard-only, reduced motion, dark/light schemes, slow network, and
 offline/failure paths when relevant.

 6. Record source URL, exact version/commit, license, notices, and dependencies for every third-party inclusion.

 7. Re-test after customization; a copied component can look correct while losing accessibility or state behavior.




                                                  End of handbook
